# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>
    sector : Sector
    sessions : List<Session>
    addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
    addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
    cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
    removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Participant {
}
abstract class Person {
    id : String
}
class School {
    name : String
    courses : List<Course>
    sectors : List<Sector>
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions
Participant --|> Person
School *-- "*" Course : courses
School *-- "*" Sector : sectors
Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
Session::replaceTrainer
Summary: Replaces the currently assigned trainer of this session with a new trainer, but only if performed strictly before the session date. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If newTrainer is null, return false.
  2. If today is null or today is not strictly before this.date (i.e., today >= this.date), return false.
  3. If this.trainer is null, return false (use assignTrainer for initial assignment).
  4. Set this.trainer = newTrainer.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: trainer (Trainer), date (LocalDate)
Related Classes: Trainer
Input: newTrainer : Trainer — the replacement trainer; today : LocalDate — the current date; format "yyyy-MM-dd".
Output: boolean — true if the trainer was replaced; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: Session exists; newTrainer is non-null; today is a valid LocalDate strictly earlier than this.date; this.trainer is currently non-null.
Post-condition: If true is returned, this.trainer == newTrainer; otherwise, this.trainer is unchanged.

Session::assignTrainer
Summary: Assigns a trainer to this session, but only if no trainer is currently assigned and the operation is performed strictly before the session date. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If t is null, return false.
  2. If today is null or today is not strictly before this.date (i.e., today >= this.date), return false.
  3. If this.trainer is not null, return false (cannot assign when one already exists; use replaceTrainer).
  4. Set this.trainer = t.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: trainer (Trainer), date (LocalDate), registeredParticipants (List<Participant>)
Related Classes: Trainer
Input: t : Trainer — the trainer to assign; today : LocalDate — the current date used to validate timing; format "yyyy-MM-dd".
Output: boolean — true if the trainer was assigned; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: Session exists; t is non-null; today is a valid LocalDate strictly earlier than this.date; this.trainer is currently null.
Post-condition: If true is returned, this.trainer == t; otherwise, this.trainer is unchanged.

Session::registerParticipant
Summary: Registers a participant for this session, ensuring no duplicate enrollment. Returns true on success, false if the participant is null, already registered, or the session date is not in the future.
Algorithm:
  1. If p is null, return false.
  2. If today is null or today is not strictly before this.date (i.e., today >= this.date), return false (registration must occur before the session date).
  3. If this.registeredParticipants already contains p, return false.
  4. Add p to this.registeredParticipants.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: registeredParticipants (List<Participant>), date (LocalDate)
Related Classes: Participant
Input: p : Participant — the participant to enroll; today : LocalDate — the current date; format "yyyy-MM-dd".
Output: boolean — true if the participant was successfully registered; false otherwise.
Pre-condition: Session exists; p is non-null; today is a valid LocalDate strictly earlier than this.date; p is not already registered.
Post-condition: If true is returned, p is in this.registeredParticipants and no duplicates exist; otherwise, this.registeredParticipants is unchanged.

```plantuml
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions
Participant --|> Person
School *-- "*" Course : courses
School *-- "*" Sector : sectors
Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
School::assignCourseToSector
Summary: Assigns a course to a new sector. The course is first removed from its current sector (if any), then added to the new sector. Returns true on success, false if the course or sector is not found.
Algorithm:
  1. Find the Course in this.courses by courseId; if not found, return false.
  2. Find the Sector in this.sectors by sectorId; if not found, return false.
  3. If the course currently has a sector (course.sector != null), call sector.removeCourse(course) on the old sector and set course.sector = null.
  4. Call newSector.addCourse(course).
  5. Return true.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>), sectors (List<Sector>)
Related Classes: Course, Sector
Input: courseId : String — the unique ID of the course to reassign; sectorId : String — the unique ID of the target sector; both must be non-null and non-empty.
Output: boolean — true if the assignment succeeded; false if either ID was not found.
Pre-condition: The school exists; both courseId and sectorId correspond to existing Course and Sector instances.
Post-condition: If true is returned, the course's sector is the new sector and it appears in that sector's courses list, and no longer appears in any previous sector's courses list; otherwise, no changes occur.

School::addCourse
Summary: Adds a new course to the school by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. Returns true on success, false if the ID is null, empty, or already used by an existing course.
Algorithm:
  1. If id is null or empty (after trim), return false.
  2. Iterate through this.courses; if any existing course has the same id (case-sensitive), return false.
  3. Create a new Course with the given id, empty documents list, null sector, and empty sessions list.
  4. Add the new Course to this.courses.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>)
Related Classes: Course
Input: id : String — the unique identifier for the new course; case-sensitive; non-null and non-empty.
Output: boolean — true if the course was added; false if the id was invalid or already existed.
Pre-condition: The school exists; id is a non-null, non-empty string.
Post-condition: If true is returned, this.courses contains a new Course with the given id and no duplicates exist by id; otherwise, this.courses is unchanged.

School::addSector
Summary: Creates a new sector with a unique ID and adds it to the school's sector list. Returns the newly created Sector, or null if the ID is invalid or already used.
Algorithm:
  1. If id is null or empty (after trim), return null.
  2. Iterate through this.sectors; if any existing sector has the same id (case-sensitive), return null.
  3. Create a new Sector with the given id and an empty courses list.
  4. Add the new Sector to this.sectors.
  5. Return the new Sector.
Related Features: sectors (List<Sector>)
Related Classes: Sector
Input: id : String — the unique identifier for the new sector; case-sensitive; non-null and non-empty.
Output: Sector — the newly created Sector instance, or null if creation failed.
Pre-condition: The school exists; id is a non-null, non-empty string.
Post-condition: If a non-null Sector is returned, this.sectors contains the new Sector; otherwise, this.sectors is unchanged.

01:40:33.760 [main] DEBUG org.springframework.web.client.DefaultRestClient -- Writing [ChatCompletionRequest[messages=[ChatCompletionMessage[rawContent=# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

Algorithm:
  1. Find the Course in this.courses by courseId; if not found, return false.
  2. Find the Sector in this.sectors by sectorId; if not found, return false.
  3. If the course currently has a sector (course.sector != null), call sector.removeCourse(course) on the old sector and set course.sector = null.
  4. Call newSector.addCourse(course).
  5. Return true.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>), sectors (List<Sector>)
Related Classes: Course, Sector
Input: courseId : String — the unique ID of the course to reassign; sectorId : String — the unique ID of the target sector; both must be non-null and non-empty.
Output: boolean — true if the assignment succeeded; false if either ID was not found.
Pre-condition: The school exists; both courseId and sectorId correspond to existing Course and Sector instances.
Post-condition: If true is returned, the course's sector is the new sector and it appears in that sector's courses list, and no longer appears in any previous sector's courses list; otherwise, no changes occur.

School::addCourse
Summary: Adds a new course to the school by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. Returns true on success, false if the ID is null, empty, or already used by an existing course.
Algorithm:

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in <Operations> with a specfication.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title trainingSchool Model

class Course {
    id : String
    documents : List<Document>
    sector : Sector
    sessions : List<Session>
    addDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
    addSession(date : LocalDate) : List<Session>
    cancelSession(date : LocalDate) : boolean
    removeDocument(doc : Document) : boolean
}
class Document {
    name : String
}
class Participant {
}
abstract class Person {
    id : String
}
class School {
    name : String
    courses : List<Course>
    sectors : List<Sector>
    addCourse(id : String) : boolean
    addSector(id : String) : Sector
    assignCourseToSector(courseId : String, sectorId : String) : boolean
}
class Sector {
    id : String
    courses : List<Course>
    addCourse(course : Course) : void
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
}
class Session {
    date : LocalDate
    registeredParticipants : List<Participant>
    trainer : Trainer
    assignTrainer(t : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    registerParticipant(p : Participant, today : LocalDate) : boolean
    replaceTrainer(newTrainer : Trainer, today : LocalDate) : boolean
}
class Trainer {
    contractor : boolean
}

Course --> "*" Document : documents
Course --> Sector : sector  'opposite: courses
Course *-- "*" Session : sessions
Participant --|> Person
School *-- "*" Course : courses
School *-- "*" Sector : sectors
Sector --> "*" Course : courses  'opposite: sector
Session --> "*" Participant : registeredParticipants
Session --> Trainer : trainer
Trainer --|> Person
@enduml
```

The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). 5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.

# Operation Specifications
Session::replaceTrainer
Summary: Replaces the currently assigned trainer of this session with a new trainer. The replacement must occur strictly before the session's date. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If newTrainer is null or today is null, return false.
  2. If trainer is null, return false (no existing trainer to replace; use assignTrainer instead).
  3. If today is not strictly before date (i.e., today >= date), return false.
  4. Set trainer = newTrainer.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: trainer (Trainer), date (LocalDate).
Related Classes: Trainer, Person.
Input: newTrainer : Trainer — the new trainer to assign; today : LocalDate — the current date used to validate the timing constraint.
Output: boolean — true if the trainer was replaced; false if newTrainer/today is null, no existing trainer, or the timing constraint is violated.
Pre-condition: The Session instance exists; the session already has a trainer assigned; today is strictly before the session's date.
Post-condition: On true, trainer is set to newTrainer; on false, trainer is unchanged.

Session::assignTrainer
Summary: Assigns a trainer to this session. The assignment must occur strictly before the session's date (same-day assignment is not allowed). Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If t is null or today is null, return false.
  2. If today is not strictly before date (i.e., today >= date), return false.
  3. If this session already has a trainer assigned, return false (use replaceTrainer for replacement).
  4. Set trainer = t.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: trainer (Trainer), date (LocalDate).
Related Classes: Trainer, Person.
Input: t : Trainer — the trainer to assign; today : LocalDate — the current date used to validate the timing constraint.
Output: boolean — true if the trainer was assigned; false if t/today is null, the timing constraint is violated, or a trainer is already assigned.
Pre-condition: The Session instance exists; the session has no trainer yet; today is strictly before the session's date.
Post-condition: On true, trainer is set to t; on false, trainer is unchanged.

Session::registerParticipant
Summary: Registers a participant for this session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If p is null, return false.
  2. If registeredParticipants already contains p (compared by id), return false (no duplicate enrollments).
  3. Append p to registeredParticipants.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: registeredParticipants (List<Participant>).
Related Classes: Participant, Person.
Input: p : Participant — the participant to register; must be non-null.
Output: boolean — true if the participant was registered; false if p is null or already enrolled.
Pre-condition: The Session instance exists; the participant is not already registered for this session.
Post-condition: On true, p is present in registeredParticipants; on false, registeredParticipants is unchanged.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
School::assignCourseToSector
Summary: Assigns a course to a new sector. The course is first removed from its current sector (if any), then added to the new sector. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If courseId or sectorId is null or empty, return false.
  2. Find the Course in courses whose id equals courseId; if not found, return false.
  3. Find the Sector in sectors whose id equals sectorId; if not found, return false.
  4. If the course's current sector is non-null and is different from the target sector, call currentSector.removeCourse(course) and set course.sector = null.
  5. Call targetSector.addCourse(course) (which sets course.sector = targetSector).
  6. Return true.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>), sectors (List<Sector>); the course's sector reference is updated.
Related Classes: Course, Sector.
Input: courseId : String — the unique id of the course to reassign; sectorId : String — the unique id of the target sector; both non-null and non-empty.
Output: boolean — true if the course was successfully assigned to the target sector; false if either id is invalid or the course/sector does not exist.
Pre-condition: The School instance exists; a course with courseId and a sector with sectorId both exist in this school.
Post-condition: On true, the course belongs to the target sector and is no longer associated with any previous sector; on false, the course's sector membership is unchanged.

School::addCourse
Summary: Adds a new Course to the school with a case-sensitive unique id. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If id is null or empty (or blank), return false.
  2. Iterate through courses; if any existing course has an id equal (case-sensitive) to id, return false.
  3. Create a new Course with the given id, empty documents, null sector, empty sessions.
  4. Append the new Course to courses.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>).
Related Classes: Course.
Input: id : String — the unique case-sensitive identifier of the course; non-null and non-empty.
Output: boolean — true if the course was added; false if id is null/empty or already exists (case-sensitive match).
Pre-condition: The School instance exists; the provided id is a non-null, non-empty string.
Post-condition: On true, a new Course with the given id is present in courses; on false, courses is unchanged.

School::addSector
Summary: Creates a new Sector with the given unique id and adds it to the school's sectors list. Returns the created Sector, or null if the id is invalid or already exists.
Algorithm:
  1. If id is null or empty (or blank), return null.
  2. Iterate through sectors; if any existing sector has an id equal to id, return null.
  3. Create a new Sector with the given id and an empty courses list.
  4. Append the new Sector to sectors.
  5. Return the newly created Sector.
Related Features: sectors (List<Sector>).
Related Classes: Sector.
Input: id : String — the unique identifier of the sector; non-null and non-empty.
Output: Sector — the newly created Sector instance, or null if the id is null/empty or already exists.
Pre-condition: The School instance exists; the provided id is a non-null, non-empty string.
Post-condition: On non-null return, the new Sector is present in sectors with an empty courses list; on null return, sectors is unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
School::assignCourseToSector
Summary: Assigns a course to a new sector. The course is first removed from its current sector (if any), then added to the new sector. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If courseId or sectorId is null or empty, return false.
  2. Find the Course in courses whose id equals courseId; if not found, return false.
  3. Find the Sector in sectors whose id equals sectorId; if not found, return false.
  4. If the course's current sector is non-null and is different from the target sector, call currentSector.removeCourse(course) and set course.sector = null.
  5. Call targetSector.addCourse(course) (which sets course.sector = targetSector).
  6. Return true.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>), sectors (List<Sector>); the course's sector reference is updated.
Related Classes: Course, Sector.
Input: courseId : String — the unique id of the course to reassign; sectorId : String — the unique id of the target sector; both non-null and non-empty.
Output: boolean — true if the course was successfully assigned to the target sector; false if either id is invalid or the course/sector does not exist.
Pre-condition: The School instance exists; a course with courseId and a sector with sectorId both exist in this school.
Post-condition: On true, the course belongs to the target sector and is no longer associated with any previous sector; on false, the course's sector membership is unchanged.

School::addCourse
Summary: Adds a new Course to the school with a case-sensitive unique id. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If id is null or empty (or blank), return false.
  2. Iterate through courses; if any existing course has an id equal (case-sensitive) to id, return false.
  3. Create a new Course with the given id, empty documents, null sector, empty sessions.
  4. Append the new Course to courses.
  5. Return true.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>).
Related Classes: Course.
Input: id : String — the unique case-sensitive identifier of the course; non-null and non-empty.
Output: boolean — true if the course was added; false if id is null/empty or already exists (case-sensitive match).
Pre-condition: The School instance exists; the provided id is a non-null, non-empty string.
Post-condition: On true, a new Course with the given id is present in courses; on false, courses is unchanged.

School::addSector
Summary: Creates a new Sector with the given unique id and adds it to the school's sectors list. Returns the created Sector, or null if the id is invalid or already exists.
Algorithm:
  1. If id is null or empty (or blank), return null.
  2. Iterate through sectors; if any existing sector has an id equal to id, return null.
  3. Create a new Sector with the given id and an empty courses list.
  4. Append the new Sector to sectors.
  5. Return the newly created Sector.
Related Features: sectors (List<Sector>).
Related Classes: Sector.
Input: id : String — the unique identifier of the sector; non-null and non-empty.
Output: Sector — the newly created Sector instance, or null if the id is null/empty or already exists.
Pre-condition: The School instance exists; the provided id is a non-null, non-empty string.
Post-condition: On non-null return, the new Sector is present in sectors with an empty courses list; on null return, sectors is unchanged.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

# Operation Specifications
Course::removeDocument
Summary: Removes a referenced Document from this course. Returns true if the document was successfully removed; otherwise, false.
Algorithm:
  1. If doc is null, return false.
  2. If documents does not contain doc (matched by name), return false.
  3. Remove doc from documents.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: documents (List<Document>).
Related Classes: Document.
Input: doc : Document — the document to remove; must be non-null and currently present in the course's documents list.
Output: boolean — true if the document was removed; false if doc is null or not present.
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists; the Document is currently attached to this course.
Post-condition: On true, the document is no longer in the documents list; on false, documents is unchanged.

Course::addDocument
Summary: Attaches a referenced Document to this course. Returns true if the document was successfully added; otherwise, false.
Algorithm:
  1. If doc is null, return false.
  2. If documents already contains doc (compared by name), return false (avoid duplicates).
  3. Append doc to documents.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: documents (List<Document>).
Related Classes: Document.
Input: doc : Document — the document to attach; must be non-null and its name should be unique within the course's document list.
Output: boolean — true if the document was added, false otherwise (null input or duplicate name).
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists; the provided Document is initialized with a non-null name.
Post-condition: When true is returned, the documents list contains the new Document; when false is returned, the documents list is unchanged.

Course::addSession
Summary: Adds a new Session to this course on the given date and returns the up-to-date list of sessions of the course.
Algorithm:
  1. If date is null, return the current sessions list unchanged.
  2. Iterate through sessions; if any session has the same date, do not add a new one and return the current sessions list.
  3. Create a new Session with the given date, empty registeredParticipants, and no trainer.
  4. Append the new Session to sessions.
  5. Return the updated sessions list.
Related Features: sessions (List<Session>).
Related Classes: Session.
Input: date : LocalDate — the date of the session in "yyyy-MM-dd" format; must be non-null.
Output: List<Session> — the up-to-date list of sessions of this course (unchanged if the date already had a session or input was invalid).
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists; the input date is a valid LocalDate.
Post-condition: If the date did not previously exist in sessions, a new Session is appended; otherwise the sessions list is unchanged.

Course::cancelSession
Summary: Cancels (removes) a session of this course on the given date, only if the session has no registered participants. Returns true on success, false otherwise.
Algorithm:
  1. If date is null, return false.
  2. Find the Session in sessions whose date equals the given date.
  3. If no such session exists, return false.
  4. If the session has registeredParticipants that is non-empty, return false (cannot cancel a session with participants).
  5. Remove the session from sessions.
  6. Return true.
Related Features: sessions (List<Session>).
Related Classes: Session, Participant.
Input: date : LocalDate — the date of the session to cancel, format "yyyy-MM-dd"; must be non-null.
Output: boolean — true if the session was removed; false if the date is null, no matching session exists, or the session still has participants.
Pre-condition: The Course instance exists; the date refers to an existing session of this course; the session (if found) has zero registered participants.
Post-condition: On true, the session no longer appears in sessions; on false, sessions is unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
Sector::addCourse
Summary: Adds a course to this sector. If the course previously belonged to another sector, it is detached from it. The course's sector reference is updated to this sector.
Algorithm:
  1. If course is null, return without action.
  2. If course.sector is non-null and is different from this sector, call course.sector.removeCourse(course) to detach it.
  3. If courses does not already contain course, append course to courses.
  4. Set course.sector = this.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>); the course's sector attribute is mutated.
Related Classes: Course, School (indirectly, via School::assignCourseToSector).
Input: course : Course — the course to attach to this sector; must be non-null.
Output: void.
Pre-condition: The Sector instance exists; the provided Course is non-null.
Post-condition: The course is present in this sector's courses list and its sector reference points to this sector.

Sector::removeCourse
Summary: Removes a course from this sector and clears the course's sector reference.
Algorithm:
  1. If course is null, return without action.
  2. Remove course from courses if present.
  3. If course.sector == this, set course.sector = null.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>); the course's sector attribute is mutated.
Related Classes: Course.
Input: course : Course — the course to detach from this sector; must be non-null.
Output: void.
Pre-condition: The Sector instance exists; the provided Course is non-null.
Post-condition: The course is no longer in this sector's courses list, and if it was previously associated with this sector, its sector reference is null.

You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pesudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Either in a school that performs the following functions: Develop catalogs that describe the courses and give the estimated dates of the sessions (date format : "2025-02-06"). Each course has an unique id. Register people have their unique id and can participate in the sessions. Determine the trainers who will lead the sessions. Each trainer has an unique id. Some sessions may be led by individual contractors. Some courses are assigned and organized into sectors which have unique ids. The courses use referenced documents with names. The system can upload/delete documents to/from courses. The school has a unique name.
Functional requirement:
1. Add a course. The school can add a new course by providing a case-sensitive unique ID. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, false.

2. Assign a course to a sector. A course can belong to exactly one sector at a time. When assigning a course to a new sector, the system must first remove it from its current sector (if any) before adding it to the new one. Return true if successful; otherwise, false.

3. Manage the session schedule of a cours. For any course, the system can add or cancel sessions by providing a specific date (format: yyyy-MM-dd). When adding, ensure only one session per course per day.  The operation returns the course's up-to-date list of sessions. When canceling, only sessions without participants may be removed. The operation returns true on success; otherwise, it returns false.

4. Assign or replace a trainer for a session. The trainer assignment or replacement must be done strictly before the session's start date (i.e., the current date must be strictly earlier than the session date; same-day operations are not allowed). The operation returns a boolean value indicating success.

5. Register a participant for a session. A participant may enroll in any session by providing a date and course ID. The call adds the participant to the specified session, ensuring no duplicate enrollments for the same participant. It returns true on success; otherwise, false.
```

```plantuml
    removeCourse(course : Course) : void
```

# Operation Specifications
Sector::addCourse
Summary: Adds a course to this sector. If the course previously belonged to another sector, it is detached from it. The course's sector reference is updated to this sector.
Algorithm:
  1. If course is null, return without action.
  2. If course.sector is non-null and is different from this sector, call course.sector.removeCourse(course) to detach it.
  3. If courses does not already contain course, append course to courses.
  4. Set course.sector = this.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>); the course's sector attribute is mutated.
Related Classes: Course, School (indirectly, via School::assignCourseToSector).
Input: course : Course — the course to attach to this sector; must be non-null.
Output: void.
Pre-condition: The Sector instance exists; the provided Course is non-null.
Post-condition: The course is present in this sector's courses list and its sector reference points to this sector.

Sector::removeCourse
Summary: Removes a course from this sector and clears the course's sector reference.
Algorithm:
  1. If course is null, return without action.
  2. Remove course from courses if present.
  3. If course.sector == this, set course.sector = null.
Related Features: courses (List<Course>); the course's sector attribute is mutated.
Related Classes: Course.
Input: course : Course — the course to detach from this sector; must be non-null.
Output: void.
Pre-condition: The Sector instance exists; the provided Course is non-null.
Post-condition: The course is no longer in this sector's courses list, and if it was previously associated with this sector, its sector reference is null.

  public boolean cancelSession(final LocalDate date)
```java
  {
```
    if (date == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    EList<Session> currentSessions = getSessions();
    Session toCancel = null;
    for (Session s : currentSessions)
    {
      if (s.getDate() != null && s.getDate().equals(date))

      if (d.getName() != null && d.getName().equals(doc.getName()))
      {
        return false;
      }
    }
    currentDocs.add(doc);
    return true;
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Removes a referenced Document from this course. Returns true if the document was successfully removed; otherwise, false.
   * Algorithm:
   *   1. If doc is null, return false.
   *   2. If documents does not contain doc (matched by name), return false.
   *   3. Remove doc from documents.
   *   4. Return true.
   * Related Features: documents (List<Document>).
   * Related Classes: Document.
   * Input: doc : Document — the document to remove; must be non-null and currently present in the course's documents list.
   * Output: boolean — true if the document was removed; false if doc is null or not present.
   * Pre-condition: The Course instance exists; the Document is currently attached to this course.
   * Post-condition: On true, the document is no longer in the documents list; on false, documents is unchanged.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model docRequired="true"
   *        annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public boolean removeDocument(final Document doc)
  {
    if (doc == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    EList<Document> currentDocs = getDocuments();
    Document toRemove = null;
    for (Document d : currentDocs)
    {
      if (d.getName() != null && d.getName().equals(doc.getName()))
      {
        toRemove = d;
        break;
      }
    }
    if (toRemove == null)
    {
      return false;
    }
    currentDocs.remove(toRemove);
    return true;
  }
